### Knowledge Base RAG / Semantic Retrieval (supporting pipeline)

**What it is:** A background embedding pipeline that chunks and vector-embeds document/file content so
Zyra's free-text AI chat can retrieve relevant knowledge-base context semantically, fused with keyword
full-text search — distinct from (and additive to) the manual keyword `search` endpoint above.

**Use cases:**
- Zyra answering a free-text question by pulling in relevant KB documents/files as context, even when the user's wording doesn't literally match the document text.
- Keeping AI context fresh automatically as documents/files are created/edited/transcribed, without any manual "reindex" action from the user.

**Problem it solves:** Plain keyword/ILIKE search misses semantically-related content that doesn't share exact wording; embeddings let Zyra find conceptually relevant KB content.

**Features & actions:**
- Every document create/update(-with-content-change)/duplicate, and every file create/transcription-completion, calls `enqueueEmbedding` (fire-and-forget, failures swallowed) which:
  - Immediately flips the source's `embedding_status` to `'queued'`.
  - Enqueues a BullMQ job keyed by a deterministic `jobId` (`document-<id>` / `file-<id>`) so concurrent enqueues for the same source dedupe (no duplicate embedding work queued).
- The embedding worker (`RagEmbeddingProcessor`):
  - Re-reads the source's current content (`content_text` for documents, `extracted_text` for files); if the row is gone/soft-deleted by the time the job runs, marks status `'unsupported'` and exits cleanly (no error).
  - Skips (marks `'unsupported'`) if content is empty, or if the project has no usable embedding-capable AI key allocation (e.g. an Anthropic-only project — Anthropic doesn't offer embeddings, so this is expected, not a bug; Zyra silently falls back to keyword search for such projects).
  - **Idempotent via content hash**: computes a SHA-256 of the content; if it matches `embedding_content_hash` already stored, skips re-embedding entirely (no-op) — re-saving a document with unchanged content_text doesn't burn embedding-API calls.
  - Chunks content (`RagChunkingService`): splits on markdown headings first (tracking a "Heading > Subheading" breadcrumb per chunk), then recursively on blank-line/newline/sentence boundaries down to a target size, merging small pieces back up with a small overlap between consecutive chunks; hard-cuts anything still oversized. Capped at a max chunk count per source (excess content is silently dropped, not erroring).
  - Replaces all prior chunks for that source in one transaction (delete + reinsert) — old chunk rows never leak/duplicate across re-embeds.
  - Chunks land in a hash-partitioned (64 buckets by `project_id`) `knowledge_document_chunks` table with an HNSW cosine-similarity index.
- Retrieval (`RagRetrievalService.retrieveKnowledgeContext`) — never throws (any failure anywhere resolves to `[]`, so callers' existing fallback logic stays clean):
  - Runs ANN (vector cosine similarity against the query's own embedding) and FTS (Postgres `plainto_tsquery` against documents and files separately) in parallel.
  - Both legs apply the **same ai_memory-approval filter** as `knowledgeSnapshot` (`document_type != 'ai_memory' OR status = 'approved'`) — unapproved AI memory is invisible here too.
  - Fuses ANN + FTS rankings via Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) in application code (not SQL, since ANN is chunk-level and FTS is document-level).
  - Returns up to `RAG_MAX_SOURCES` items, trimmed to fit a total character budget (`RAG_CONTEXT_CHAR_BUDGET`), each with a citation (`sourceType`, `sourceId`, `headingPath`).
- Self-healing: `resumeInterruptedEmbeddings` re-enqueues anything stuck in `pending/queued/processing` (e.g. after a crash/restart) — safe to call repeatedly due to the deterministic jobId.
- This pipeline is internal/AI-facing only — there is no direct user-facing endpoint or UI surface for "semantic search"; it's consumed by Zyra's chat feature (outside this module's scope) rather than the Knowledge Base search box.

**Key files:**
- `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-ingestion.service.ts` (`enqueueEmbedding`, `resumeInterruptedEmbeddings`)
- `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-embedding.processor.ts` (worker: content-hash idempotency, chunk+embed+store)
- `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-chunking.service.ts` (heading-aware recursive chunking)
- `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-retrieval.service.ts` (ANN+FTS fusion, ai_memory approval filter, char-budget trimming)
- `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag.constants.ts`, `rag.types.ts`
- Schema: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/migrations/V55_knowledge_document_chunks.sql` (64-way hash-partitioned table + HNSW index), `V56_knowledge_embedding_status.sql`
- Enqueue call sites in `legacy.service.ts`: `enqueueEmbedding` wrapper (328), and call sites at 3253, 3332, 3375, 3526, 3600, 4444, 4816, 6539, 6547

### Knowledge Base v1 (legacy flat items — superseded)

**What it is:** The original flat (non-hierarchical) knowledge base model (`knowledge_base_items`: notes
and files with no folders), left routable for backward compatibility but not used by the current
frontend, which is built entirely against the v2 (folders/documents/files) API.

**Use cases:** None in current UI — these routes are dead code from the frontend's perspective, kept only for API/rollback safety after the V45 migration moved data into the new schema.

**Problem it solves:** N/A currently — historical only.

**Features & actions:**
- `GET /knowledge-base` (`listKnowledge`) — list notes/files for a project, optional `type`/`search` (ILIKE on title/content) filters; **no auth/project-membership check** on this handler (`@Param`/`@Query` only, no `@Req`) — unlike every v2 endpoint, this route doesn't call `requireProjectAccess`, meaning (if still reachable) any authenticated-or-not caller who knows a `projectId` could list its legacy notes. Worth a security-focused test: confirm whether this route is actually reachable in production or effectively dead/blocked upstream.
- `POST /knowledge-base` (`createKnowledge`) — creates an `item_type='note'` row; defaults title to "Untitled note".
- `GET /knowledge-base/:itemId` (`getKnowledge`) — **no project-scoping at all**: looks up purely by `itemId` with no `projectId`/`is_deleted` check in the WHERE clause and no auth check — an item from Project A is fetchable via Project B's URL, or without matching project at all. Flag as a potential IDOR-style test case even though the feature is legacy/likely unused by the UI.
- `PATCH /knowledge-base/:itemId` (`updateKnowledge`) — same lack of scoping; updates title/content only.
- `DELETE /knowledge-base/:itemId` (`deleteKnowledge`) — hard delete (no soft-delete column on this table at all), same lack of scoping.
- `POST /knowledge-base/upload` — stubbed, always returns `{ error: "File uploads are not enabled in this endpoint yet" }` regardless of input.
- `GET /knowledge-base/:itemId/file` — stubbed, always returns `{}`.
- Route-ordering note (called out in the controller's own comment): the v2 literal routes (`folders`, `search`, `documents`, `files`) must be declared before these v1 `:itemId` catch-all routes, otherwise Express/Nest would match e.g. `GET /knowledge-base/search` against `GET /knowledge-base/:itemId` (with `itemId="search"`) instead of the intended search handler. Worth a regression test that hitting `/knowledge-base/search`, `/knowledge-base/folders`, `/knowledge-base/documents`, `/knowledge-base/files` never accidentally falls through to the v1 `:itemId` handlers.

**Key files:**
- Backend controller: `legacy.controller.ts` lines 969–1002
- Backend service: `listKnowledge` (2793), `createKnowledge` (2808), `getKnowledge` (2817), `updateKnowledge` (2823), `deleteKnowledge` (2830)
- Schema: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/migrations/V14_knowledge_base_items.sql`
- No frontend usage found (superseded by v2 pages/components documented above).

## Section H — Third-Party Integrations (Jira, Linear, OAuth)

Scope note on naming: `src/auth/api-token.service.ts` ("Tesbo MCP API tokens") issues Tesbo's *own*
bearer tokens so external/machine clients can call *Tesbo's* REST API without a browser session. It
has nothing to do with Jira or Linear. Historically there was also a per-workspace "Personal Access
Token" path for connecting Jira/Linear (`auth_method = 'personal_token'`); that has been removed —
see the compatibility note below.

### Workspace Integration Connections (OAuth Framework)

**What it is:** A generic, provider-parameterized framework (`:provider` = `jira` or `linear`) for
connecting a third-party issue tracker to a Tesbo workspace via the standard OAuth 2.0
authorization-code flow. The connection is organization/workspace-scoped (one connection per
provider per workspace), not per-project — a workspace owner connects Jira/Linear once, and
individual projects later opt in by mapping to a remote project/team (see the Jira/Linear
Integration sections).

**Exactly one way to configure it.** OAuth client credentials come from the backend environment
(`JIRA_CLIENT_ID`/`JIRA_CLIENT_SECRET`, `LINEAR_CLIENT_ID`/`LINEAR_CLIENT_SECRET`) and nothing else.
There is no per-workspace override and no personal-token alternative. Tesbo Cloud and a self-hosted
install run the *identical* flow; they differ only in whose OAuth app the credentials belong to —
Tesbo's for Cloud, the operator's own for self-hosted. A workspace owner's entire job is clicking
**Connect**.

**Use cases:**
- A workspace owner connects Jira or Linear once for the whole workspace instead of every project
  owner authenticating separately.
- An operator (Tesbo Cloud, or anyone self-hosting) registers one OAuth app for the deployment so
  every workspace on it connects in one click.

**Problem it solves:** Avoids re-authenticating per project, avoids storing raw OAuth credentials in
plaintext, and removes OAuth-app registration from the end user's path entirely.

**Compatibility note (breaking, for anyone upgrading):** two previously-supported connection paths
were removed.
- Per-workspace OAuth app config (`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/config`).
  Any leftover `integration_oauth_configs` rows are now **ignored** — the table is no longer read.
  A workspace that had configured its own app must have the deployment's env credentials set instead.
- Personal Access Token auth (`POST /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/connect-token`). This was
  the only path that worked for **Jira Server / Data Center**, which cannot use Atlassian cloud
  OAuth — those instances can no longer be connected at all. Existing rows with
  `auth_method = 'personal_token'` will no longer authenticate correctly, since Jira requests now
  always go through the `api.atlassian.com/ex/jira` gateway addressed by `cloud_id` rather than the
  customer's own site URL with HTTP Basic.
The `auth_method` / `personal_token_identifier` columns and the `integration_oauth_configs` table
are intentionally left in the schema (no migration drops them), so the change is reversible and any
rows in an existing deployment are preserved rather than destroyed.

**Features & actions:**
- **View config status** — `GET /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/config` — returns just
  `configured` (are env credentials present), `clientId`, and `redirectUri`. Read-only; there is
  nothing to write. Not owner-gated — any workspace member can see whether the deployment is set up.
  `redirectUri` is returned even when unconfigured, so a self-hosted operator can see which callback
  URL to register in the provider console.
- **Get the OAuth authorize URL** — `GET /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/auth-url` — builds
  the provider-specific authorization URL (Atlassian: `auth.atlassian.com/authorize` with
  `audience=api.atlassian.com` and scope `read:jira-work read:jira-user write:jira-work
  offline_access`; Linear: `linear.app/oauth/authorize` with scope `read,write,issues:create,comments:create`).
  Both set a **signed** `state` shaped `<provider>.<base64url payload>.<base64url HMAC-SHA256>`,
  where the payload carries `{p: provider, o: organizationId, t: issuedAt, n: nonce}`. The leading
  provider segment is what the callback page reads to know which provider redirected back; the
  signature is verified server-side at the callback (see below). The signing key is derived as
  `sha256("tesbo:oauth-state:" + SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY)` rather than reusing that key directly.
  - **Owner-gated** — a non-owner gets a 403 before any authorize URL is issued.
  - Throws a 400 naming the required env vars ("… is not configured on this deployment. Set
    `JIRA_CLIENT_ID` and `JIRA_CLIENT_SECRET` in the backend environment and restart.") when
    credentials are absent. This is deliberately operator-facing: no end user can fix it from the UI.
  - `*_REDIRECT_URI` is **optional** — only `*_CLIENT_ID` + `*_CLIENT_SECRET` are required, and the
    redirect defaults to `FRONTEND_URL` (or `APP_URL`, else `http://localhost:1010`) +
    `/integrations/callback`. Set it explicitly only when the browser-facing callback host differs
    from `FRONTEND_URL`.
  - Unsupported `:provider` values (anything other than `jira`/`linear`) are rejected with 400
    *before* any DB query.
  - **Not** plan-gated, unlike the callback — see the gap noted at the end of this section.
- **OAuth callback / token exchange** — `POST /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/callback` —
  exchanges the `code` for tokens and upserts `integration_connections` (`ON CONFLICT
  (organization_id, provider) DO UPDATE`, so reconnecting the same provider updates the same row
  rather than creating a duplicate — existing project mappings/synced tickets tied to that
  connection id remain intact). Always written with `auth_method = 'oauth'`.
  - Owner-only (403 for any other role), and plan-gated via `planLimits.assertIntegrationAllowed`.
  - Requires `code` in the body (400 if missing).
  - Requires the signed `state` from the authorize URL, forwarded verbatim by the callback page.
    Rejected with 400 when it is missing, unsigned/forged, tampered with after signing, minted for a
    different provider, or older than its 10-minute TTL; a validly-signed state belonging to a
    *different* workspace fails with "This authorization was started for a different workspace."
    Because all workspaces on a deployment share one `client_id`, this binding is what stops an
    attacker from handing a workspace owner a callback URL bearing the attacker's `code` and
    grafting the attacker's Jira/Linear site onto the victim's workspace. State is verified
    **before** the token exchange, so a bad state never reaches the provider. The check is stateless
    (HMAC + TTL, no nonce store), so a state is workspace-bound and time-limited but **not** strictly
    single-use within its TTL — worth a test if replay-within-window matters.
  - Jira: posts to `auth.atlassian.com/oauth/token`; requires both `access_token` and
    `refresh_token` in the response (400 "Jira did not return OAuth tokens." if either is missing);
    then calls `accessible-resources` to discover the Jira Cloud site — 400 "No accessible Jira
    site was found." if the resource list is empty (e.g. user granted OAuth consent but the app
    isn't installed on any site).
  - Linear: posts to `api.linear.app/oauth/token` (form-encoded); requires `access_token` (400 if
    absent); then queries `organization { id urlKey }` over GraphQL — 400 "Could not read the
    connected Linear workspace." if `urlKey` is missing.
  - Access/refresh tokens are encrypted (AES-256-GCM) before insert; `token_expires_at` is computed
    from the provider's `expires_in` (default 3600s for Jira, ~10 years for Linear since Linear's
    OAuth tokens are treated as effectively non-expiring by this integration).
  - Any upstream non-2xx response from Jira/Linear surfaces as a 400 with the truncated response
    body as `detail`.
- **Disconnect** — `DELETE /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/disconnect` — owner-only; deletes
  the `integration_connections` row for that provider/workspace.
  - **Cascades**: `jira_project_mappings`/`linear_project_mappings` and `jira_tickets`/
    `linear_tickets` all have `ON DELETE CASCADE` FKs to `integration_connections.id`, so
    disconnecting silently deletes *every* project's mapping and *all* synced ticket cache rows for
    that provider workspace-wide, even if 10 different projects had it mapped.
  - Does **not** cascade to: test cases' `jira_issue_key`/`jira_url`/`linear_issue_key`/`linear_url`
    fields (plain text columns, no FK — they remain pointing at a key that may no longer resolve to
    a live synced ticket), bugs' `integration_provider`/`integration_issue_key` fields, or
    Knowledge Base documents previously mirrored from synced tickets (`knowledge_documents` with
    `source_provider = 'jira'/'linear'`, also no FK to the connection). Reconnecting starts sync
    from a clean slate and project-to-remote mappings must be re-selected from scratch.
- **Connection status** — `GET /api/workspace/integrations/:provider/status` — returns
  `connected`, connection `id`, `siteUrl`, `tokenExpiresAt`, `connectedBy`, `createdAt`, and
  `connectedProjects` (every Tesbo project currently mapped to this connection, with name/key).
  Not owner-gated.

**Known gap worth testing:** `planLimits.assertIntegrationAllowed` runs in the *callback* but not in
*auth-url*, so a workspace whose plan disallows integrations can click Connect, complete the
provider's consent screen, and only then be rejected. Fail-late rather than fail-fast.

**Key files:**
- Backend controller: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.controller.ts`
  (`auth-url`, `config` GET, `callback`, `disconnect`, `status`)
- Backend service: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.service.ts` — `integrationConfigStatus`,
  `integrationAuthUrl`, `integrationCallback`, `integrationDisconnect`, `integrationStatus`, private
  helpers `envIntegrationConfig`, `integrationOAuthConfig` (env-only, synchronous),
  `defaultIntegrationRedirectUri`, `getIntegrationConnection`, `jiraBaseUrlAndAuth`,
  `linearAuthHeader`, module-level `signOAuthState`/`verifyOAuthState`/`oauthStateKey`, constants
  `JIRA_OAUTH_SCOPE`/`LINEAR_OAUTH_SCOPE`, `assertIntegrationProvider`
- Crypto: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/common/crypto.util.ts` (AES-256-GCM `encryptSecret`/`decryptSecret`)
- Migrations: `V38_jira_oauth_config.sql`, `V47_integration_connections.sql` (workspace-scoped
  tables + migration of legacy per-project connections), `V68_integration_personal_access_tokens.sql`
  — the latter two now describe columns/tables the code no longer reads (see the compatibility note)
- Tests: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/linear-integration.spec.ts` (env-only config resolution,
  state signing/verification, owner gating, malformed-response handling)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/components/settings/IntegrationsTab.tsx` (workspace Integrations tab —
  connect/manage/disconnect cards, connected-project count),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/components/integrations/WorkspaceIntegrationConfig.tsx` (Connect/Disconnect only;
  when unconfigured it renders operator instructions naming the two env vars and the callback URL to
  register, since no end user can fix it),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/settings/integrations/{page,jira/page,linear/page}.tsx`,
  `Tesbo-Frontend/app/integrations/callback/page.tsx` (OAuth redirect landing page),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` (`getIntegrationConfig`, `getIntegrationAuthUrl`,
  `integrationCallback`, `getIntegrationStatus`, `disconnectIntegration`)

### Jira Integration

**What it is:** Project-scoped mapping, ticket sync, ticket browsing, and commenting on top of a
workspace's shared Jira connection. Connecting Jira (above) is workspace-wide; each Tesbo project
separately picks which Jira project(s) feed it.

**Use cases:**
- A QA lead maps their Tesbo project to the matching Jira project(s) so tickets show up as
  Requirements/Knowledge Base context.
- Pulling the latest Jira tickets into Tesbo to see test coverage (which tickets have a linked test
  case, which don't) without leaving Tesbo.
- Posting a comment back to a Jira issue directly from Tesbo (e.g. noting which test cases cover
  it) without switching tools.
- Searching for a specific Jira issue live (not from the sync cache) when linking a bug or test
  case to a ticket that was just filed and hasn't synced yet.

**Problem it solves:** Gives testers/PMs a coverage view (Jira tickets vs. test cases) and a way to
close the loop (commenting) without needing a Jira license/tab open, while keeping ticket data fresh
via on-demand sync rather than continuous webhooks.

**Features & actions:**
- **Connection status** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/jira/status` — `connected`, connection
  `id`, `cloudId`, `siteUrl`, `tokenExpiresAt`, `connectedBy`, `createdAt`, and
  `connectedProjects` = the Jira project(s) currently mapped to *this* Tesbo project (from
  `jira_project_mappings`, `enabled = true`).
- **List remote Jira projects** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/jira/projects` — live call to
  `{baseUrl}/rest/api/3/project/search?maxResults=100`; 404 "Jira is not connected." if the
  workspace has no Jira connection; each item flagged `connected: true/false` based on whether it's
  already mapped to this Tesbo project. Filters out any Jira project missing an `id` or `key`.
  - Capped at 100 results — a Jira instance with >100 projects will have some projects unreachable
    from this picker (no pagination implemented); worth testing against a large Jira site.
- **Save Jira project mapping** — `POST /api/projects/:projectId/jira/projects` with
  `{ projects: [{id,key,name}, ...] }`.
  - **Full replace, not merge**: deletes all existing mappings for
    `(projectId, connectionId)` then re-inserts the submitted set — unchecking a previously-mapped
    project and saving unlinks it.
  - Entries missing `id` or `key` are silently dropped (not an error).
  - Submitting an empty `projects` array clears all mappings for the project (unlinks everything) —
    the *backend* allows this, but the *frontend* Save button is disabled when zero items are
    selected (`selected.size === 0`), so unlinking every Jira project can only be done today by
    checking a different item then unchecking it, or by calling the API directly. Worth testing
    both the UI restriction and the raw API behavior.
  - 404 "Jira is not connected." if there's no workspace Jira connection yet.
- **Sync tickets** — `POST /api/projects/:projectId/jira/sync` (`{ synced: <count> }`).
  - No-op (`{ synced: 0 }`, no error) if no Jira project is mapped to this Tesbo project yet.
  - For every mapped Jira project key, runs `project = "KEY" ORDER BY updated DESC` (JQL) with
    `maxResults: 100` per project and **no pagination beyond the first 100 issues per project** —
    a mapped Jira project with >100 issues will not have all of them synced in one call; there's no
    incremental/paged sync today. Good boundary test: map a project with >100 issues and confirm
    only the newest 100 (by `updated`) land.
  - Upserts each issue into `jira_tickets` keyed on `(jira_connection_id, jira_issue_id, project_id)`
    — re-running sync updates existing rows in place (status/summary/etc. refresh) rather than
    duplicating.
  - **Also mirrors every synced ticket into the Knowledge Base's "Requirements" folder** as a
    `knowledge_documents` row (`document_type='requirement_note'`, `source_provider='jira'`,
    `source_external_id=<jira issue id>`) and enqueues it for embedding/RAG — so Jira tickets become
    searchable Zyra context, not just a coverage list. If the project's Requirements folder was
    deleted/renamed, sync falls back to the project's root Knowledge Base folder; if there's no root
    folder at all, the mirror step is silently skipped for that sync run (tickets still land in
    `jira_tickets`, just not mirrored to Knowledge Base) — worth testing a project with a
    missing/renamed Requirements folder.
  - Jira's rich-text (Atlassian Document Format) `description` field is flattened to plain text via
    `jiraDescriptionToText` — nested formatting/tables/media are lost in the mirrored copy; a ticket
    with complex ADF content (tables, embedded images, mentions) is a good edge case to visually
    diff against the original Jira ticket.
  - Any single malformed/unreachable Jira API response aborts the whole sync (no partial-success
    reporting beyond "synced so far" — since it's a straight `await` loop, an error on project 2 of
    3 leaves project 1's tickets synced but throws before project 3 runs).
- **Browse synced tickets** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/jira/tickets` — paginated (`limit`
  capped at 100, default 25; `offset`), filterable by free-text `search` (matches issue key or
  summary, case-insensitive), `issueType`, `status`, and `coverage` (`covered`/`uncovered`, based on
  whether any non-deleted test case in the project has a matching `jira_issue_key`). This reads the
  local sync cache (`jira_tickets`), not live Jira — data can be stale until the next Sync.
- **Add a comment to a Jira issue** — `POST /api/projects/:projectId/jira/comment` with
  `{ issueKey, comment }` (aliases `jiraIssueKey`/`body` also accepted). Both required (400
  otherwise). Posted as a single-paragraph Atlassian Document Format comment via
  `POST {baseUrl}/rest/api/3/issue/{issueKey}/comment`. 404 if Jira isn't connected for the project's
  workspace. Edge cases: commenting on an issue key that doesn't exist / was deleted upstream
  (upstream 404 surfaces as a Tesbo 400), commenting with a token/user that lacks comment
  permission on that specific issue (permission scheme edge case even though the workspace-level
  connection is valid).
- **Live issue search** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/jira/search-issues?search=...` —
  deliberately bypasses the `jira_tickets` sync cache (comment in code: "used by the bug-linking
  picker, where a ticket filed moments ago may not have synced yet"); builds a JQL restricted to the
  project's mapped Jira project keys, matching `summary ~ "term*"` or an exact `key = "TERM"` (case
  is upper-cased for the key match); returns up to 20 results with `{provider, key, summary, status,
  url}`. `{ list: [] }` (not an error) if no Jira project is mapped yet.

**Key files:**
- Backend controller: `legacy.controller.ts` lines 1045–1078
- Backend service: `legacy.service.ts` — `jiraStatus` (~4268), `jiraProjects` (~4290),
  `connectJiraProjects` (~4309), `syncJira` (~4334), `jiraTickets` (~4451), `jiraComment` (~4485),
  `jiraSearchIssues` (~4511), `getJiraConnection` (~4546), `jiraBaseUrlAndAuth` (~4588), `jiraFetch`
  (~4608), `jiraDescriptionToText` (~190s)
- Migrations: `V12_jira_integration.sql`, `V47_integration_connections.sql` (mapping/ticket tables
  now FK to the shared connection)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/settings/integrations/jira/page.tsx`,
  `Tesbo-Frontend/components/integrations/ProjectIntegrationMapping.tsx` (shared mapping/sync UI for
  both providers), `Tesbo-Frontend/components/IssuePickerModal.tsx` (search-issues consumer),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` (`getJiraStatus`, `listJiraProjects`, `connectJiraProjects`,
  `syncJiraTickets`, `listJiraTickets`, `addJiraComment`, `searchJiraIssuesLive`),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/requirements/page.tsx` (ticket list + coverage stat strip
  consumer, cross-source with Linear)

### Linear Integration

**What it is:** The Linear mirror of the Jira integration above — project-scoped mapping, sync,
ticket browsing, and commenting — structurally identical but built on Linear's GraphQL API and
"team" (not "project") as the unit of work.

**Use cases:** Same as Jira — mapping a Tesbo project to Linear team(s), pulling issues in for
coverage tracking, commenting back to Linear from Tesbo, live-searching Linear issues when linking
a bug/test case.

**Problem it solves:** Same as Jira, for teams whose issue tracker is Linear instead of/alongside Jira.

**Features & actions:**
- **Connection status** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/linear/status` — `connected`, `id`,
  `siteUrl`, `tokenExpiresAt`, `connectedBy`, `createdAt`, `connectedProjects` = mapped Linear teams
  for this Tesbo project (from `linear_project_mappings`).
- **List remote Linear teams** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/linear/teams` — GraphQL
  `query { teams { nodes { id key name } } }` (no pagination arguments passed — relies on Linear's
  default page size, so a Linear workspace with a very large number of teams may not return all of
  them; worth a boundary test). 404 "Linear is not connected." if not connected. Each team flagged
  `connected: true/false`.
- **Save Linear team mapping** — `POST /api/projects/:projectId/linear/teams` with
  `{ projects: [{id,key,name}, ...] }` (same field name `projects` reused for teams, mirroring the
  Jira endpoint's shape). Same **full-replace** semantics as Jira (delete-then-insert), same
  silent-drop of entries missing `id`/`key`, same "empty array clears mapping" backend behavior with
  the same frontend Save-button restriction (disabled at 0 selected). Verified by
  `linear-integration.spec.ts`: malformed entries (empty id, empty key) are dropped and only
  well-formed teams are linked; submitting zero valid teams still clears old mappings via the
  DELETE but issues no INSERT.
- **Sync tickets** — `POST /api/projects/:projectId/linear/sync` (`{ synced: <count> }`).
  - No-op if no team is mapped.
  - Per mapped team, fetches up to 100 issues (`first: 100, orderBy: updatedAt`) — same "no
    pagination past the first 100" caveat as Jira; a team with >100 issues won't fully sync in one
    call.
  - Upserts into `linear_tickets` keyed on `(integration_connection_id, linear_issue_id)`; sets a
    fixed `issue_type = "Issue"` for every row (Linear has no separate "issue type" taxonomy the way
    Jira does — every synced item is typed "Issue"), pulls `state.name` as `status`,
    `priorityLabel` as `priority`.
  - Also mirrors each synced issue into the Knowledge Base's "Requirements" folder
    (`source_provider='linear'`) and enqueues embedding, with the same Requirements-folder-missing
    fallback-to-root behavior as Jira.
- **Browse synced tickets** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/linear/tickets` — same
  pagination/search/filter shape as Jira's tickets endpoint (`limit`/`offset`/`search`/`issueType`/
  `status`/`coverage`), reading the `linear_tickets` cache.
- **Add a comment to a Linear issue** — `POST /api/projects/:projectId/linear/comment` with
  `{ issueKey, comment }` (alias `linearIssueKey`). Both required. First resolves the issue by
  `issueKey` via `query Issue($id: String!) { issue(id: $id) { id } }` (falls back to using the raw
  `issueKey` as the id if the lookup returns nothing) then runs `commentCreate` mutation. 404 if
  Linear isn't connected. Edge case: commenting with an issue identifier Linear can't resolve (e.g.
  a stale/typo'd key) — the fallback silently tries to comment using that string as an ID, which
  will likely fail upstream with a GraphQL error surfaced as a 400.
- **Live issue search** — `GET /api/projects/:projectId/linear/search-issues?search=...` — for each
  mapped team, runs a GraphQL query with `filter: { title: { containsIgnoreCase: search } }` (or no
  filter if search is blank), first 20 per team, returns `{provider: "LINEAR", key, summary,
  status, url}`. `{ list: [] }` if no team mapped.

**Key files:**
- Backend controller: `legacy.controller.ts` lines 1082–1115
- Backend service: `legacy.service.ts` — `linearStatus` (~4636), `linearTeams` (~4658),
  `connectLinearTeams` (~4677), `syncLinear` (~4703), `linearTickets` (~4823), `linearComment`
  (~4946), `linearSearchIssues` (~4965), `linearGraphQL` (~4617), `linearAuthHeader` (~4603)
- Migration: `V47_integration_connections.sql` (defines `linear_project_mappings`, `linear_tickets`)
- Tests: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/linear-integration.spec.ts` (`connectLinearTeams`
  malformed-entry and empty-selection behavior; `linkedLinearKeys` aggregate)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/settings/integrations/linear/page.tsx`,
  `Tesbo-Frontend/components/integrations/ProjectIntegrationMapping.tsx` (shared with Jira),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` (`getLinearStatus`, `listLinearTeams`, `connectLinearTeams`,
  `syncLinearTickets`, `listLinearTickets`, `addLinearComment`, `searchLinearIssuesLive`)

### Ticket Linking (Test Cases & Bugs)

**What it is:** The mechanisms by which a Tesbo test case or bug record points at a specific Jira or
Linear ticket. There is no dedicated "ticket link" table for test cases — a test case simply carries
plain `jira_issue_key`/`jira_url`/`linear_issue_key`/`linear_url` columns (one Jira key + one Linear
key max per test case). Bugs similarly carry `integration_provider`/`integration_issue_key`
columns, plus a separate `bug_links` table that links a bug to a Tesbo *test case/cycle/execution*
(not to an external ticket) — the two link concepts are easy to confuse and worth testing
independently.

**Use cases:**
- Tagging a test case with the Jira/Linear requirement it was written to cover, so Requirements-page
  coverage stats and the "linked-jira-keys"/"linked-linear-keys" aggregates work.
- When a scripted test execution fails, filing a bug and recording where that defect is *actually*
  tracked — either by picking an already-filed Jira/Linear ticket via live search, or by pasting a
  URL to wherever the tester will log it themselves.
- Linking a failed execution to a Tesbo bug that already exists (not a new bug), to avoid duplicate
  bug reports for the same underlying defect across multiple runs.

**Problem it solves:** Connects the "what was tested" (test case/execution) side of Tesbo to the
"what's the actual requirement/defect" side (Jira/Linear), enabling coverage reporting and reducing
duplicate bug filing.

**Features & actions:**
- **Test case ↔ ticket link** — set directly on `POST /api/projects/:projectId/testcases` and
  `PATCH /api/testcases/:id` via `jiraIssueKey`/`jiraUrl`/`linearIssueKey`/`linearUrl` body fields
  (no dedicated endpoint or validation that the key actually exists in `jira_tickets`/
  `linear_tickets`, or that the URL matches the key — these are free-text fields set by whatever UI
  calls update, so a test case can reference a ticket key that was mistyped, belongs to an
  unmapped/different Jira project, or no longer exists upstream, without any error).
  - `GET /api/projects/:projectId/testcases` supports filtering by `jiraIssueKey`/`linearIssueKey`
    query params (exact match).
  - `GET /api/projects/:projectId/testcases/linked-jira-keys` and `.../linked-linear-keys` —
    aggregate every distinct linked key across a project's non-deleted test cases with a per-key
    count (`{ keys: [...], counts: { KEY: n } }}`) — used to show "this ticket already has N test
    cases" in ticket-linking UIs. A deleted test case (`deleted_at` set) is excluded, so
    soft-deleting the last test case linked to a key drops that key out of the aggregate.
  - Test case table UI shows the linked Jira key as a clickable link (icon + key, opens `jiraUrl` in
    a new tab) when both key and URL are present, or as plain unlinked text if only the key is set
    (no URL) — worth testing a test case with a key but no URL, and vice versa (URL but no key,
    which the UI doesn't render at all since it branches on `jiraIssueKey` first).
- **"Link a ticket" search picker** (`IssuePickerModal`) — used when filing a bug from a failed test
  execution. Defaults to whichever provider (Jira or Linear) is connected for the project;
  if both are connected, the user can flip between "Jira"/"Linear" tabs. Debounced (300ms) live
  search via `jiraSearchIssuesLive`/`searchLinearIssuesLive`. Shows "No issue tracker is connected
  for this project" if neither is connected. Selecting a result shows `{key} — {summary}` with an
  ✕ to clear and re-search.
- **File a bug from a failed execution, linking to an existing external ticket** (cycle/run detail
  page): "Is this defect already logged?" → "Yes, link existing" → choose Jira ticket / Linear
  ticket / an existing Tesbo bug.
  - Choosing "Existing Tesbo bug" opens a different picker (`ExistingBugPickerModal`) and calls
    `POST /api/bugs/:bugId/links` (`addBugLink`) with `{testcaseId, cycleId, executionId}` — this
    attaches the *current failing execution* to an already-existing bug record (`bug_links` table,
    `ON CONFLICT (bug_id, testcase_id, cycle_id) DO NOTHING`) rather than creating a new bug. This is
    the correct path for "same defect, another failed run."
  - Choosing "Jira ticket"/"Linear ticket" opens `IssuePickerModal` scoped to that provider; picking
    a result stores it in local component state (`bugIssue`) and displays `{key} — {summary}` in the
    dialog. **However, as currently written, the "File Bug" submit handler
    (`handleBugSubmit` in `app/(app)/projects/[id]/cycles/[cycleId]/page.tsx`) hard-codes
    `integrationIssueKey: null` in the `createBug` payload regardless of what was picked** — the
    same is true of the create/edit bug forms on the standalone Bugs page
    (`app/(app)/projects/[id]/bugs/page.tsx`, both `integrationIssueKey: null`). The picked ticket
    key is shown on screen but does not appear to be persisted to the bug record in either flow.
    **This is a high-value, concrete thing to verify end-to-end**: file a bug, mark it "already
    logged" in Jira/Linear, pick a specific ticket via search, save, then re-open the bug and
    confirm whether the Jira/Linear ticket key is actually stored and displayed (`viewBug.integrationIssueKey`
    on the bug detail view falls back to `viewBug.externalUrl` when the key is empty, which would
    mask the gap unless specifically checked).
- **File a bug, self-logging it in an external tracker (no ticket search)** — "Where should this be
  tracked?" → "I'll log it in my task management system myself" → pick Jira/Linear/Other +
  paste a URL (`SelfLoggedTrackerField` — deliberately has no search, per an explicit code comment:
  "the ticket already lives in the user's own tracker, we're just recording where to find it").
  Sets `externalUrl` + `integrationProvider` (`JIRA`/`LINEAR`/`OTHER`) on the bug; `integrationIssueKey`
  stays null by design here (only a URL is captured, not a parsed key) — do not conflate this
  intentional gap with the search-picker gap above; they are two different code paths with
  different (and differently justified) behavior.
  - No URL format validation observed beyond the `type="url"` HTML input — pasting a non-URL string
    is a plausible edge case (stored as-is in `external_url`).
  - Bug detail view labels the external link "{Provider} Ticket" when `integrationProvider` is set,
    or generic "Bug Link" otherwise, and displays `integrationIssueKey || externalUrl` as the link
    text — since `integrationIssueKey` is always null from every current UI path, this always
    falls through to showing the raw URL.
- **Requirements page coverage view** (cross-source): `GET /api/projects/:projectId/tickets/summary`
  and `GET /api/projects/:projectId/tickets` aggregate Jira + Linear synced tickets into one
  paginated, filterable (search/issueType/status/coverage) list with total/covered/uncovered counts
  and a coverage percentage bar, per source and combined. "Covered" is computed live via an `EXISTS`
  check against `testcases.jira_issue_key`/`linear_issue_key` — so linking/unlinking a test case's
  ticket key immediately moves it between covered/uncovered on next load, with no caching lag. (The
  "Generate testcase coverage" AI action available from this page belongs to the AI test-generation
  feature, out of scope here — only the ticket list/coverage/filtering itself is part of this
  integrations surface.)

**Key files:**
- Backend: `legacy.controller.ts` — `linkedJiraKeys`/`linkedLinearKeys` routes (lines 263–270),
  bug links routes (`POST /api/bugs/:bugId/links`, `DELETE /api/bugs/:bugId/links/:linkId`, lines
  481–488); `legacy.service.ts` — `createTestCase` (~1348), `updateTestCase` (~1394),
  `listTestCases` filter columns (~1262), `linkedJiraKeys` (~1487), `linkedLinearKeys` (~1496),
  `createBug` (~1903), `getBug` (~1936), `updateBug` (~1943), `addBugLink` (~1966), `removeBugLink`
  (~1976), `requirementsSummary` (~4910), `allTickets` (~4860)
- Migrations: `V13_testcase_jira_link.sql`, `V66_testcase_linear_link.sql` (adds the plain
  `jira_issue_key`/`linear_issue_key`/`*_url` columns to `testcases`, with a partial index on
  non-null keys)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/components/IssuePickerModal.tsx` (Jira/Linear live search picker),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/components/SelfLoggedTrackerField.tsx` (manual URL/system entry, no search),
  `Tesbo-Frontend/components/TrackingDestinationField.tsx` ("Log into Tesbo" vs "I'll log it myself"
  toggle), `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/cycles/[cycleId]/page.tsx` (file-bug-from-execution
  dialog — contains the `integrationIssueKey: null` gap noted above), `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/bugs/page.tsx`
  (standalone Bugs list/create/edit — same gap), `Tesbo-Frontend/components/testcases/RepositoryTestCaseTable.tsx`
  (Jira-key column rendering), `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/requirements/page.tsx`
  (cross-source coverage view), `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` (`linkedJiraKeys`/`linkedLinearKeys`,
  `addBugLink`, `createBug`, `updateBug`)

## Section I — Platform Admin, Branding, Tesbo Reports, MCP Server, RAG Pipeline

### Super Admin Console

**What it is:** A platform-wide admin console, separate from any single customer's workspace, restricted to users listed in the `platform_admins` table. It gives Tesbo's own operators (not customers) visibility into system health and control over who else holds this cross-tenant privilege.

**Use cases:**
- Tesbo's ops/founder team checking that the API, database, and email provider are healthy right after a deploy.
- Onboarding a new internal teammate as a platform admin so they can access `/admin`.
- Auditing who currently holds platform-admin rights and who granted it to them.
- (Latent) Pulling a cross-tenant view of every customer organization's size/activity for account management — endpoint exists but has no UI yet (see gap below).

**Problem it solves:** Tesbo is multi-tenant (organizations/workspaces are customers). Regular workspace owners can only manage their own organization; the Super Admin console is the only place with visibility/control that spans every organization in the database, and it needs to be locked down independently of any one workspace's role model.

**Features & actions:**
- **Access gating**
  - Entire `/admin` area is client-gated: `AdminLayout` calls `authMe()` and redirects to `/projects` unless `isPlatformAdmin` is true on the session (`GET /api/auth/me`).
  - Every backend admin route independently re-checks membership in `platform_admins` (`requirePlatformAdmin` in `legacy.service.ts`, and `SuperAdminService.isPlatformAdmin` for the health controller) — the frontend gate is not the only line of defense.
  - Two internal roles exist on `platform_admins.role`: `owner` and `admin`. Only `owner` can add/remove other admins; a plain `admin` can view but not manage (`AdminsTab.tsx` gates the add-form and remove-button on `role === "owner"`, but this is a UI-only check — worth testing that the backend also rejects a non-owner `admin` calling POST/DELETE `/api/admin/admins`, since `addAdmin`/`deleteAdmin` in `legacy.service.ts` only call `requirePlatformAdmin`, not an owner-specific check).
  - `deleteAdmin` guards `role <> 'owner'` in its SQL `WHERE` clause, so an owner row can never be deleted through this endpoint (test: attempt to delete the seed owner and confirm no-op / still present).
- **System Health page** (`/admin`, default landing page)
  - Fetches `GET /api/admin/system/health`: reports database status + latency + latest applied migration filename, and email provider (Postmark) "configured" vs "not_configured" state. Overall `status` is `"degraded"` if any service reports an error.
  - Manual "Refresh" button and an "Auto-refresh (30s)" checkbox that polls on an interval.
  - Renders one status card per service with a colored status dot (green pulsing = up, amber = misconfigured/warning, red = down/error), latency, provider, migration id, and any error text.
  - Edge cases to test: DB unreachable (should surface `services.database.status = "error"` with the caught error message, overall `status: "degraded"`, not a 500); Postmark token unset (should read "not_configured" rather than erroring).
- **Deployment branding editor** (embedded in the same System Health page — see Platform Branding section below for the underlying feature; the admin page is one of its two write surfaces along with... actually its only write surface).
- **Manage Admins page** (`/admin/admins` in the sidebar, but that route itself immediately client-redirects to `/settings?tab=admins` — the actual UI lives in the workspace Settings modal/tab, rendered by `AdminsTab.tsx`)
  - List all platform admins: name/avatar initials, email, role badge (`owner` vs `admin`), who granted them access and when (`GET /api/admin/admins`).
  - Add a platform admin by email (owner-only in the UI): the target user must already have a Tesbo account — `addAdmin` calls `upsertUser(email)` and then inserts/upgrades a `platform_admins` row (`ON CONFLICT (user_id) DO UPDATE SET role = 'admin'`), so re-adding an existing admin is idempotent and does not create a duplicate row. Test: adding an email with no existing user account — does `upsertUser` create a stub user, and can that user actually authenticate afterward?
  - Remove a platform admin (owner-only in the UI, and owner rows are protected server-side as noted above).
  - Empty state ("No platform admins configured") when the list is empty.
- **Customers overview (backend-only, currently unreachable from the UI)**
  - `GET /api/admin/customers` returns, per organization across the whole platform: id, name, slug, created_at, member count, project count, test case count, automated-test count and coverage %, and last-activity timestamp — plus a `summary` block (totals). This is a genuine cross-tenant query (`FROM organizations o LEFT JOIN organization_members ... LEFT JOIN projects ... LEFT JOIN testcases`).
  - **Gap worth flagging to QA:** there is no `/admin/customers` (or similarly named) page in the frontend, no nav item in `AdminSidebar.tsx`, and no `lib/api.ts` client function calling this route at all. The endpoint is fully implemented and platform-admin-gated but currently dead from a user-journey standpoint — it can only be exercised via a direct authenticated HTTP call. Also note the `summary` block in the service code looks partially unfinished: `totalMembers` and `totalAutomated` are hardcoded to `0` and `overallAutomationCoverage` to `0` regardless of actual data, which would fail any assertion expecting real aggregate numbers there.
- **Sidebar/session**
  - `AdminSidebar.tsx` shows a "Platform Admin" badge, links to System Health and Manage Admins, a collapse/expand toggle, "Back to Workspace" (→ `/projects`), and Log out.

**Key files:**
- Backend: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/admin/admin-health.controller.ts` (`GET /api/admin/system/health`), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/admin/super-admin.service.ts` (`isPlatformAdmin`), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/admin/admin.module.ts`
- Backend (legacy): `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.controller.ts` lines 1147–1180 (`/api/admin/customers`, `/api/admin/admins` GET/POST, `/api/admin/admins/:adminId` DELETE); `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.service.ts` — `adminCustomers()` (~line 3836), `adminList()` (~line 3870), `addAdmin()` (~line 3928), `deleteAdmin()` (~line 3940), `requirePlatformAdmin()` (~line 363)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/app/admin/layout.tsx`, `Tesbo-Frontend/app/admin/page.tsx` (System Health + branding editor), `Tesbo-Frontend/app/admin/admins/page.tsx` (redirect shim), `Tesbo-Frontend/components/AdminSidebar.tsx`, `Tesbo-Frontend/components/settings/AdminsTab.tsx`
- Frontend API client: `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` — `getSystemHealth`, `getAdminList`, `addPlatformAdmin`, `removePlatformAdmin` (no client function exists for `/api/admin/customers`)

### Platform Branding

**What it is:** A single, platform-wide (not per-customer) product name + logo override, stored in a `platform_settings` key/value row, shown across every unauthenticated and authenticated surface of the app (login/signup, invite pages, shared-report pages, the main app sidebar).

**Use cases:**
- Tesbo (as the platform operator) white-labels the product name/logo for a self-hosted or reseller deployment without a code change or redeploy.
- Reverting a mis-applied logo back to the shipped Tesbo default in one click.

**Problem it solves:** Lets deployment-level branding (product name + logo) be changed at runtime by a platform admin, without needing engineering involvement, and centralizes it so every page that shows a logo/name stays in sync.

**Features & actions:**
- **Public branding read** — `GET /api/branding` (no auth required): returns `{ productName, logoUrl }`, falling back to `"Tesbo Test Manager"` / `/tesbo-test-manager-logo.png` if no `platform_settings` row exists yet or the query itself fails (query wrapped in `.catch()`), so branding never blocks page render.
  - Consumed by the `BrandLogo` component used on: login/auth split-screen (`AuthSplitShell.tsx`), workspace setup page, invite + invite-register pages, public shared test-run page (`/share/[token]`), and the main app `Sidebar.tsx`.
- **Admin branding read** — `GET /api/admin/branding`: platform-admin-gated version of the same payload (functionally identical to the public one today; exists as a separate authenticated surface for the admin editor).
- **Update branding** — `PATCH /api/admin/branding` (platform-admin only):
  - `productName`: trimmed; empty string silently falls back to `"Tesbo Test Manager"` rather than erroring.
  - `logoUrl`: must be either a same-origin public asset path (starts with `/`) or a base64 data URL with an allowed image mime type (`png`, `jpe?g`, `webp`, `svg+xml`) — anything else (e.g. an external `https://` image URL) is rejected with `400 Logo must be an uploaded image data URL or a public app asset path.` Test: pasting a remote image URL should fail, not silently pass through.
  - Size guard: rejects any logo payload over 2,500,000 characters (~2 MB) with `400 Logo is too large. Upload an image below 2 MB.` — note the UI's own client-side file-size check (`file.size > 2MB`) is on the *raw file*, while the backend check is on the *base64-encoded string length*, which is ~33% larger than the raw bytes; a file just under 2 MB raw could still be rejected server-side after base64 encoding — worth testing at the boundary.
  - Persisted via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE` on `platform_settings`, recording `updated_by`.
- **Admin UI** (embedded at the top of the System Health page, `/admin`):
  - Live logo preview, product-name text field, "Upload logo" file picker (accepts png/jpeg/svg/webp; client-side rejects non-image files and files >2MB before even reading them), a rendered preview once a file is chosen, "Save branding" and "Reset default" buttons, and inline success/error messaging.
  - "Reset default" restores the shipped name + logo path and immediately persists it (not just a local UI reset).

**Key files:**
- Backend: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.controller.ts` lines 1157–1170 (`GET /api/branding`, `GET /api/admin/branding`, `PATCH /api/admin/branding`); `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.service.ts` — `publicBranding()` (~3891), `adminBranding()` (~3900), `updateAdminBranding()` (~3905)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/app/admin/page.tsx` (branding section of System Health page), `Tesbo-Frontend/components/BrandLogo.tsx` (consumer used across auth/invite/share/sidebar), `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` (`getBranding`, `getAdminBranding`, `updateAdminBranding`, `BrandingSettings` type)

---

### Tesbo Reports (Automated Test Run Ingestion)

**What it is:** A designed-but-not-yet-implemented module intended to let external CI pipelines (Playwright first, per the client code) push automated test-run results into a project for reporting/analytics/alerting, kept separate from Tesbo's manual test-case/cycle-execution workflow. **As it exists in this codebase today, this is a stub**: the backend only implements six read-only GET endpoints that return hardcoded empty/placeholder data with no database backing, and the corresponding frontend has a fully-typed API client for a much larger surface (ingestion, alert CRUD, run sharing, settings, key rotation) but **no page or component anywhere calls any of it** — it is unreachable from the actual app UI.

**Use cases (intended, per the frontend contract — not currently functional):**
- A CI pipeline runs Playwright and uploads the JSON results (or raw result file) to Tesbo after each build, so a QA/dev lead can see pass/fail trends without leaving Tesbo.
- Comparing automated run history per spec file and per individual test to spot flaky tests.
- Getting alerted (immediate or daily digest) when a failure ratio crosses a threshold, or when a new build's status changes.
- Sharing a single automated run externally via a public, time-limited link (e.g. to a stakeholder without a Tesbo login).

**Problem it solves (intended):** Gives CI-driven automated test output a home in Tesbo distinct from manually-tracked test cases/cycles/executions, so "was last night's Playwright run green" is answerable without a separate CI dashboard.

**Features & actions:**
- **Implemented today (backend stubs, all under `/api/projects/:projectId/tesbo-reports/...`, no auth/param validation, no DB queries):**
  - `GET /runs` → always returns `[]`
  - `GET /specs` → always returns `[]`
  - `GET /tests` → always returns `[]`
  - `GET /analytics` → always returns `{ totalRuns: 0, totalTests: 0, passRate: 0, byStatus: {}, runsByDay: [] }`
  - `GET /alerts` → always returns `[]`
  - `GET /settings` → always returns `{ keepTrace: true, traceRetentionDays: 14, ingestionApiKey: "", alertsEnabled: false, shareByDefault: false }`
  - None of these six take `projectId` into account in the handler at all (the route param is declared but unused) — every project sees the identical empty payload.
- **Designed but NOT implemented anywhere in the backend (the frontend `lib/api.ts` calls these; they will 404 or fail if invoked):**
  - `GET /runs/:runId` — single run detail (spec count + per-case results with duration, trace/screenshot/video URLs, browser/OS metadata, step-level detail)
  - `GET /specs/:specName` — per-spec breakdown of its tests with pass/fail/skip counts
  - `GET /specs/:specName/tests/:testName` — history of one test across runs
  - `POST /alerts`, `PUT /alerts/:alertId`, `DELETE /alerts/:alertId`, `POST /alerts/:alertId/toggle`, `POST /alerts/:alertId/send-test` — full alert-rule CRUD; a rule has `conditionType` (`FAILURE_RATIO` | `PASS_RATIO` | `BUILD_UPDATE`), `comparator` (`GREATER_THAN` | `GREATER_OR_EQUAL`), numeric `threshold`, `recipients` (emails), `frequency` (`IMMEDIATE` | `DAILY`), `enabled`
  - `GET/POST/DELETE /runs/:runId/share` — enable/disable a public share token for one run, with an expiry (`expiresInHours`, default 168h/7 days)
  - `GET /public/tesbo-reports/:token` — the actual public (unauthenticated) view of a shared run
  - `PUT /settings` and `POST /settings/rotate-key` — update retention/alerting/sharing defaults and rotate the project's ingestion API key
  - `POST /ingest/playwright` — JSON-body ingestion of a Playwright result payload, returns `{ runId }`
  - `POST /ingest/playwright/upload` — multipart file upload variant of the same, bearer-token authenticated via `localStorage` token
  - `POST /runs/:runId/cases/:caseId/artifacts/:kind/upload` — attach a trace/screenshot/video artifact file to a specific case result
- **Nothing in `Tesbo-Frontend/app` or `Tesbo-Frontend/components` references any `tesbo-reports` path or type** (`TesboRunSummary`, `listTesboRuns`, etc.) — confirmed via full-tree search. There is no visible nav entry, project page, or route for this feature today.

**What QA should actually test/track here:** Given the above, the realistic test plan for this "feature" right now is a **negative/regression check** — confirm the six stub GET endpoints keep returning their fixed empty payloads (as a contract other future work might rely on) and confirm none of the unimplemented write/ingest/share endpoints have silently appeared as functional without corresponding release notes. This should be flagged to the product owner as an incomplete/parked feature rather than something to write full functional test cases against yet — since there is no ingestion mechanism, "runs" can never exist, so downstream analytics/alerts/sharing cannot be exercised end-to-end regardless of what the client code implies.

**Key files:**
- Backend: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.controller.ts` lines 1182–1210 (the six stub routes; handlers return literals directly in the controller, not via `LegacyService`)
- Backend: no `legacy.service.ts` methods exist for this feature at all (grep for `tesbo-reports`/`TesboReport` in the service file returns nothing)
- Frontend client (fully speculative surface, ~lines 2666–2965 of `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts`): types `TesboRunSummary`, `TesboRunCase`, `TesboRunDetail`, `TesboPublicRunDetail`, `TesboSpecSummary`, `TesboSpecDetail`, `TesboProjectTest`, `TesboAnalytics`, `TesboAlertRule`, `TesboShareState`, `TesboSettings`; functions `listTesboRuns`, `getTesboRun`, `listTesboSpecs`, `getTesboSpec`, `getTesboTestHistory`, `listTesboTests`, `getTesboAnalytics`, `listTesboAlertRules`, `createTesboAlertRule`, `updateTesboAlertRule`, `deleteTesboAlertRule`, `toggleTesboAlertRule`, `sendTesboAlertTest`, `getTesboRunShare`, `createTesboRunShare`, `disableTesboRunShare`, `getPublicTesboRun`, `getTesboSettings`, `updateTesboSettings`, `rotateTesboIngestionKey`, `ingestTesboPlaywright`, `ingestTesboPlaywrightUpload`, `uploadTesboCaseArtifact`
- No frontend page/component consumes any of the above (verified: no matches for `tesbo-reports`, `TesboRun*`, etc. anywhere under `Tesbo-Frontend/app` or `Tesbo-Frontend/components`)

### MCP Server Integration

**What it is:** An in-process, dependency-free MCP (Model Context Protocol) server exposed over a single JSON-RPC 2.0 HTTP endpoint per project, letting external AI tools/agents (e.g. Claude Desktop, or any MCP-compatible client) read and write a Tesbo project's test data using a project-scoped API bearer token instead of a browser session.

**Use cases:**
- A developer wires Claude (or another MCP client) to their Tesbo project so an AI agent can pull the current test-case list, generate new test cases from a spec, or check the requirement/traceability matrix without leaving their coding assistant.
- A CI or automation script uses a `write`-scoped token to record execution results (pass/fail) or file a bug automatically after a check.
- An AI coding agent creates a test plan/cycle for a feature it just implemented and marks off its own manual verification.

**Problem it solves:** Gives AI agents and other machine clients a safe, auditable, protocol-standard way to interact with a specific Tesbo project's data — writes are clearly attributed to a dedicated "tesbo-mcp" actor (distinct from human and other-agent activity in audit history), and a token can never act outside its own project no matter what the client requests.

**Features & actions:**
- **Transport & auth**
  - Project endpoint: `POST /api/projects/:projectId/mcp`, plus token-project alias `POST /api/mcp`; body is JSON-RPC 2.0 (`{jsonrpc:"2.0", id, method, params}`).
  - Requires `Authorization: Bearer <token>` resolved to an API token principal by `AuthMiddleware`; a browser session (cookie-based) is explicitly rejected with `401 MCP requires a valid API token` — MCP is a machine-only surface, never usable from a logged-in browser tab directly.
  - **Project-scope enforcement:** the token's own `projectId` (baked in at token creation) must equal the `:projectId` in the URL, or the call fails with JSON-RPC error code `-32001` (`ProjectScopeDenied`) — even if a caller correctly guesses/knows another project's id, a token literally cannot act outside the project it was minted for. Tested explicitly: token with no project scope at all is also rejected.
  - **Tool-scope enforcement:** each tool declares `requiredScope: "read"` or `"write"`; the token must carry that scope (tokens can hold `read`, `write`, or both) or the call fails with code `-32002` (`ScopeDenied`), naming the missing scope in the error message.
- **Protocol methods** (`tools/list`, `tools/call`, `initialize`, `ping`)
  - `initialize` → supports `2025-06-18` and `2024-11-05` (the latter is the fallback), server name `tesbo-mcp`, version `0.1.0+<deploy>.<tool-fingerprint>`, and capabilities (`tools.listChanged: false`). Returns `mcp-session-id`; stale schema sessions get HTTP 404 and must reinitialize. Initialized notifications get 202; GET/DELETE get 405 (no SSE/session termination).
  - `ping` → empty result, for client keep-alive/liveness checks.
  - `tools/list` → returns every registered tool's `name`, `description`, and JSON-Schema `inputSchema`: **50 tools, 20 read / 30 write** (source/compiled registry inventory reconciled 2026-11-12, not a new expansion in this batch).
  - `tools/call` → dispatches to the named tool with `arguments`; unknown tool name → `MethodNotFound` (-32601); on success wraps the JSON-stringified result as an MCP `content: [{type:"text", text}]` part with `isError:false`; on a handler exception, maps the underlying error (e.g. a `BadRequestException`'s message) onto a `ToolExecutionError` (-32003) rather than leaking a raw 500.
  - Malformed request (missing/wrong `jsonrpc` version, non-string `method`) → `InvalidRequest` (-32600); the request `id` is always echoed back on error responses (including `null` if none was sent), per JSON-RPC spec.
- **Exposed registry** (thin adapters over the existing services, not independent product workflows):
  - Project: `list_projects`.
  - Test cases: `list_testcases`, `get_testcase`, `create_testcase`, `update_testcase`, `archive_testcase`, `restore_testcase`, `duplicate_testcase`, `bulk_create_testcases`, `bulk_update_testcases`, `bulk_archive_testcases`, `get_testcase_bugs`, `get_testcase_executions`, `link_requirement_to_testcase`, `unlink_requirement_from_testcase`.
  - Suites: `list_suites`, `get_suite`, `create_suite`, `update_suite`, `clone_test_suite`.
  - Runs/results: `list_test_cycles`, `get_test_cycle`, `create_cycle_from_plan`, `create_cycle_from_testcases`, `record_execution_result`, `list_executions`, `get_execution`, `update_execution_result`, `bulk_record_execution_results`, `get_test_execution_summary`.
  - Bugs/traceability: `list_bugs`, `get_bug`, `create_bug`, `update_bug`, `link_testcase_to_bug`, `unlink_testcase_from_bug`, `get_requirement_matrix`.
  - Knowledge Base: `search_knowledge_base`, `list_knowledge_documents`, `get_knowledge_document`, `create_knowledge_document`, `update_knowledge_document`, `move_knowledge_document`, `archive_knowledge_document`, `restore_knowledge_document`, `list_knowledge_folders`, `get_knowledge_folder`, `create_knowledge_folder`, `update_knowledge_folder`, `move_knowledge_folder`.
  - Testcase writes use the dedicated MCP actor. Suite access checks, bug reporters, execution results and KB writes use the token's human owner where existing user-FK/role checks require it. MCP bulk tools retain explicit per-item outcomes, unlike whole-selection atomic REST bulk mutations.
- **Actor attribution model:** only write tools resolve/attach an actor id (reads never touch actor columns); the "tesbo-mcp" agent actor id is looked up once from `actors ⋈ agents` (seeded migration V65) and cached for the life of the process.
- **Edge cases validated by the existing test suite** (`mcp.service.spec.ts`) worth including in a manual QA pass too: non-2.0 payload rejected; unknown method/tool rejected; read tool works with read-only token but write tool is blocked for a read-only token; a client-supplied project id in tool arguments is ignored in favor of the token's own project; underlying service exceptions are mapped to a clean tool error rather than crashing the RPC call.

**Auth prerequisite — Personal Access Token / API token system (shared with direct REST API access, not just MCP):**
  - Tokens live in an `api_tokens` table, always project-scoped (not workspace-wide), created via `POST /api/projects/:id/apikeys` (name + optional `scopes` array, defaults to `["read","write"]` if none/invalid given), listed via `GET /api/projects/:id/apikeys` (returns only a masked `tsbo_…<last6ofhash>` prefix, never the secret again), revoked via `DELETE /api/projects/:id/apikeys/:keyId`.
  - Raw token format: `tsbo_` + 24 random bytes (hex); only a SHA-256 hash is ever stored; the raw value is returned exactly once at creation time and is unrecoverable afterward — losing it means issuing a new token.
  - `last_used_at` is stamped best-effort on every successful authentication (used for both REST bearer-auth and MCP).
  - **Existing UI:** **Project settings → API & MCP** offers named token creation, Read/Write selection, one-time reveal, masked listing and revocation, plus connection snippets. A freshly revealed token remains in snippets until reload; clear/reload or mask secrets before captures. Building/testing this flow does not establish a deployed endpoint or a connection from an owner's external client.

**Key files:**
- Backend (MCP protocol): `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/mcp/mcp.controller.ts` (scoped endpoint and alias), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/mcp/mcp.service.ts` (JSON-RPC engine, scope/membership checks, fingerprint sessions), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/mcp/mcp.tools.ts` (50-tool registry), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/mcp/mcp.types.ts` (protocol types, error codes), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/mcp/mcp.module.ts`
- Backend tests: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/mcp/mcp.service.spec.ts`
- Backend (token issuance, shared with REST bearer auth): `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/auth/api-token.service.ts` (`ApiTokenService` — `issueToken`, `listTokens`, `revokeToken`, `authenticate`), routes in `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.controller.ts` (~lines 216–229: `GET/POST /api/projects/:id/apikeys`, `DELETE /api/projects/:id/apikeys/:keyId`), handlers in `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.service.ts` (`listApiKeys`, `createApiKey`, `revokeApiKey`, ~lines 308–326), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/auth/auth.middleware.ts` (bearer-token resolution)
- Frontend: `Tesbo-Frontend/app/(app)/projects/[id]/settings/api-tokens/page.tsx`, settings `page.tsx`, `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts`.

### RAG Pipeline (Internal Infrastructure)

**What it is:** Internal plumbing — not a user-facing page in its own right — that turns a project's Knowledge Base documents/files into searchable vector embeddings, and serves hybrid semantic + keyword retrieval to power Zyra's (Tesbo's AI assistant) free-text chat context. It has no dedicated UI, but its behavior is indirectly observable (embedding status on KB documents) and its prerequisite (a workspace AI key) is configurable by workspace owners.

**What it enables:** When Zyra's chat is asked a free-text question, instead of relying only on a cheap recency-based snapshot of recently-touched KB documents, RAG retrieval finds the KB chunks that are actually semantically relevant to the question (via vector similarity) and blends them with full-text keyword matches, so Zyra's answers can cite the right section of the right document even in a large knowledge base. If nothing is embedded yet, or the project has no usable (OpenAI) AI key allocated, retrieval silently returns nothing and the caller falls back to the existing simpler snapshot — RAG is designed to never be a hard dependency or a new failure mode.

**Pipeline stages:**
1. **Ingestion (producer)** — `RagIngestionService.enqueueEmbedding()` is fired (fire-and-forget) from every KB write path in `legacy.service.ts` that creates/updates a `knowledge_documents` row or a `knowledge_files` row (including after audio/video transcription completes). It flips the source's `embedding_status` to `queued` and enqueues a BullMQ job (queue name `knowledge-embedding`) with a deterministic job id (`{sourceType}-{sourceId}`) so concurrent enqueues for the same document dedupe automatically. Jobs retry up to 3 times with exponential backoff (starting 5s).
   - Self-healing: on backend boot, `RagModule.onModuleInit` calls `resumeInterruptedEmbeddings()`, which re-enqueues anything left in `pending`/`queued`/`processing` (covers a backend crash mid-job, since Redis persistence alone doesn't recover an in-flight job the process died holding).
2. **Chunking** — `RagChunkingService.chunk()` (pure, synchronous, unit-testable, no I/O): splits text on markdown headings first (tracking a heading breadcrumb like "Setup > Prerequisites" per chunk for citation), then recursively splits each section on a separator cascade (blank line → newline → sentence → hard character cut) merging adjacent small pieces up to a ~1600-char target with a 240-char overlap between consecutive chunks (so context isn't lost at a boundary). Chunks under 20 chars are discarded; capped at 500 chunks per source document.
3. **Embedding (consumer/worker)** — `RagEmbeddingProcessor` (a BullMQ `WorkerHost`): loads the source's raw text, resolves the project's AI key allocation (`resolveEmbeddingAllocation`), and skips (marks `unsupported`) if there's no content, no allocation, or the allocated provider isn't OpenAI (Anthropic has no embeddings endpoint, so an Anthropic-only workspace key makes RAG a no-op for that project, not an error). Computes a SHA-256 content hash and skips re-embedding entirely if content is unchanged since the last successful embed. Calls `embedTexts()` (OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small`, 1536 dimensions) in batches of 96 chunks, then atomically replaces that source's rows in `knowledge_document_chunks` (delete-then-insert inside one DB transaction) with `pgvector` embedding columns. Any chunk whose returned vector doesn't match the expected 1536 dimensions is silently dropped rather than stored malformed.
   - Status lifecycle observable on the KB document/file row: `pending → queued → processing → ready`, or `unsupported` (no content / no valid key / provider mismatch) / `failed` (exhausted all 3 retries, tracked via the BullMQ `failed` worker event).
4. **Retrieval** — `RagRetrievalService.retrieveKnowledgeContext(projectId, query)`, called from Zyra's chat handler in parallel with the existing cheap "recency" knowledge snapshot (never adds latency to that path since both run concurrently); its result supersedes the fallback only if non-empty. Never throws — any failure (no allocation, embeddings API error, etc.) is caught and resolves to `[]`.
   - Runs an ANN (approximate nearest neighbor) cosine-similarity search over `knowledge_document_chunks` restricted to the project's partition (top 40 candidates) **and** a Postgres full-text search (`ts_rank`/`plainto_tsquery`) over both `knowledge_documents` and `knowledge_files` (top 20 each) in parallel.
   - Fuses the two rank lists via **Reciprocal Rank Fusion** (RRF, k=60) computed in application code (chunk-level ANN results and document-level FTS results don't reconcile cleanly in SQL).
   - Trims the fused, ranked sources down to at most 8 sources and a 6,000-character total context budget, truncating the last included source's content with a trailing `...` if it would overflow the budget.
   - AI-memory-type KB documents are excluded from both ANN and FTS retrieval unless their `status = 'approved'` — i.e. an unreviewed AI-generated memory note can't leak into Zyra's context via RAG before a human approves it.
5. **AI key allocation (`rag-ai-allocation.ts`)** — shared helper (also reused by `LegacyService.zyraAiAllocation` for Zyra generally): looks up `project_ai_key_allocations ⋈ workspace_ai_keys` for the project, and only returns a usable allocation if the key is `is_active` and its `provider` is (case-insensitively) `"openai"`. `embedTexts()` builds the request with the allocation's configurable auth header/scheme and base URL (defaults to `https://api.openai.com/v1/embeddings`, with normalization to append `/v1/embeddings` or `/embeddings` as needed for custom base URLs), and surfaces the provider's own error message on a non-2xx response.

**What's configurable / observable by a user (indirectly, through the adjacent "AI Providers" workspace settings feature, not through RAG itself):**
- Workspace owners manage one or more workspace-level AI keys (`Settings → AI Providers` tab) — name, provider (OpenAI/Anthropic/custom with configurable base URL + auth header/scheme), the secret key itself (shown masked after creation, e.g. `sk-...abcd`), and a default model — via `POST/GET/DELETE /api/workspace/ai-keys`.
- Owners allocate exactly one workspace AI key per project (a dropdown per project row); RAG (and Zyra generally) is "blocked"/no-op for a project with no allocation or a non-OpenAI allocation. This is the single lever that turns semantic KB retrieval on or off for a given project — worth a QA test matrix: no key allocated / OpenAI key allocated / Anthropic-only key allocated / custom-provider key allocated (should all degrade gracefully rather than error).
- Embedding status per KB document/file (`pending/queued/processing/ready/unsupported/failed`) is stored on the document/file row itself, so if the Knowledge Base UI (documented elsewhere) surfaces that status field, it's a directly observable signal of this pipeline's health for a given document — worth confirming whether that status is actually surfaced anywhere in the KB UI, since it wasn't in this investigation's scope to check the KB frontend.

**Key files:**
- Backend: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-ingestion.service.ts` (producer/enqueue + startup resume), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-chunking.service.ts` (pure text chunker), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-embedding.processor.ts` (BullMQ worker: embed + persist), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-retrieval.service.ts` (hybrid ANN+FTS retrieval with RRF fusion), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag-ai-allocation.ts` (`resolveEmbeddingAllocation`, `embedTexts`, `normalizeEmbeddingsUrl`), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag.constants.ts` (all tunables: chunk sizes, batch size, ANN/FTS candidate counts, RRF k, context budget), `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag.types.ts`, `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/rag/rag.module.ts`
- Integration point in host service: `Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/legacy/legacy.service.ts` — `enqueueEmbedding()` wrapper (~line 328) called from every KB create/update path (~lines 3253, 3332, 3375, 3526, 3600, 4444, 4816, 6539, 6547); retrieval call inside Zyra chat handling (~line 5336: `this.ragRetrieval.retrieveKnowledgeContext(projectId, message)`, merged with `knowledgeSnapshot()` fallback)
- Adjacent, user-facing configuration surface (separate feature, documented for cross-reference only): `Tesbo-Frontend/components/settings/AiProvidersTab.tsx` (workspace AI key CRUD + per-project allocation UI), `Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts` (`listWorkspaceAiKeys`, `createWorkspaceAiKey`, `deleteWorkspaceAiKey`, `allocateWorkspaceAiKeyToProject`, `WorkspaceAiKey`/`WorkspaceAiProjectAllocation` types), backend route family `/api/workspace/ai-keys` in `legacy.controller.ts`/`legacy.service.ts`
